The spine and a portion of the boards are covered in dark brown, aged leather with five raised bands visible on the spine. A small, blue-bordered rectangular label is attached to the lower section of the spine. The remainder of the cover consists of polished wooden boards with a visible natural grain and several knots. On the right edge of the wooden board, two metal hinges or clasp attachments are visible.</image-desc>

<image-desc>At the top of the page, a horizontal row of six waterfowl, including geese and ducks, stands on a grassy strip punctuated by red and blue flowers. Between the two columns of text on the right side of the page, there are two pairs of long-legged wading birds: an upper pair of grey herons or cranes and a lower pair of storks, one black and one white. Along the bottom margin, another horizontal row features five birds: a peacock on the left, followed by two different species of waterfowl, a dark bird of prey, and a small brownish bird, possibly a shorebird.</image-desc>

A sequence of four scientific bird illustrations spans the bottom of the page. 
1. "Corlinus": A large wading bird with a long neck and mottled brown and grey plumage, standing on a grassy patch with small red flowers.
2. "vultur": A vulture with dark grey/black feathers and a distinctive bald pink head and neck.
3. "pellicanus": A white pelican depicted in flight or a dynamic pose with its long neck tucked back towards its body and wings partially outstretched.
4. "airo": A tall, slender grey heron or egret standing in profile among sparse vegetation. 
Small plants with red and green details are scattered under the birds' feet.

<image-desc>
A vertical sequence of four bird illustrations on the right side of the page:
1. Top: A falcon (identified by the marginal note "falco") with brown and white mottled plumage and yellow talons, perched on a pinkish rock.
2. Middle: A hawk (identified by the marginal note "accipiter") with greyish barred feathers on its breast and yellow legs, perched on a green, moss-covered rock.
3. Lower Middle: Another bird of prey, similar in posture to a hawk or falcon, with brown-grey feathers, perched on a circular green mound.
4. Bottom: A long-legged wading bird, likely a heron (Ardea), with greyish plumage and a long beak, standing on level ground.

A medieval manuscript page featuring two columns of Latin text in a Gothic script. The margins are decorated with naturalistic illustrations of birds:
- Left margin: Two long-legged wading birds, a heron (labeled "arrones" in text) and another grey bird, stand on a strip of grass with red and white flowers.
- Bottom: Three waterfowl near a body of water; a brown bird on the left bank, a multi-colored duck swimming in blue wavy lines, and a dark bird on the right bank.
- Right margin: A large dark bird of prey perched on a red and white ringed pole. Below it, a smaller bird of prey on a simple perch. Portions of other birds are visible on the far right edge.

Falcones gentiles absolute an sunt sauri secundum maneriem plumagii trium modorum sunt sicut peregrini. & qui plus tendit ad illa duo saltim ad plumagium & formam falconis peregrini bone forme laudabilior habebitur. *hec de quantitate cibi dicta sunt*

### Capitulum de uicibus cibandi ip̄os & de horis conuenientib[us] cibandi

De numero autem cibādi ip̄os & de horis conuenientib[us] cibandi ip̄os dicitur. Isti pulli nouiter geniti & iūiores cum non habeantur sic parui nisi in fine neris. expectarent q[uo]d saltē dies sunt longi cibandi erunt omni die bis ad minus. Nam si semel t[ant]m daretur eis cibus non sufficeret p[ropte]r longitudinem dierū & fortem calorem quem habent. & si multum daretur semel calor diet debilitaret stomachū & indigestionem pareret p[ropte]r multitudinem cibi. Vis itaq[ue] debet q[uo]d secunda uice nō detur nisi q[uo]d prima uice sumptū sit digestū finit. apposito enim cibi sup[er] inceptam digestiōē nō digeritur naturalium uirtutum ordinem p[er]turbarer & op[er]ationes confunderer. Digestū antē cibi signa erunt in h[ui]u[s]modi auibus flatus exiens p[er] os non ferebit grauiter de cibo nichil tenebunt in <term>gorgia</term>. Imo postq[uam] cibus a <term>gorgia</term> descenderit ad stomachum & a stomacho ad intestina. et dū i[n] eisdem steterint egeret pluries & maxime f[m] q[uo]d conuenit alitari assumptoz aloy. & si egestione quam faciunt illud nigrū

<column-break/>

durum quod consueuit esse in ea erit modicū & granulosum. et illud album molle quod consueuit esse in ea erit multū liquidum & multum album. & <term>egestio</term> non erit fetida. Signa uero indigesti cibi sunt contraria p[re]dictis. Nam flatus erit grauis & si aliquid tenent in <term>gorgia</term> nōdum digesserunt. & postq[uam] cibus totaliter descenderit abis de <term>gorgia</term> ad uentrem & intestina & pluries egessissent. non tenē digesserunt. Si nigrum i[n] <term>egestiōe</term> fuerit multū & liquidum molle uero finit fetidum ualide & in q[ua]ntitate minus q[ua]m in <term>egestione</term> digesta. multiplicatio antez nigri & diminutio albi m <term>egestiōe</term> accidit p[er] calorem sapientem digerere. & non proficientem <term>digōnē</term> quod contingit uidere in igne sapiente dequoquere lapides in fornace calcinaria. qui in principio demigraritur. Si p[re]uenerint ad <term>digōnē</term> p[er]fettā nigrum minoratur. & albū multiplicatur.

<image-desc>An illumination at the bottom of the page depicts the process of capturing falcons from a mews at night, corresponding to the text above. On the left, two figures approach a stone building; the figure in front wears a blue tunic and white apron, holding a long burning candle to provide light. Behind them follows another attendant. To the right, a falconer dressed in a long red robe and a white cap reaches into a small arched doorway of the mews to retrieve a bird. The mews is a sturdy rectangular structure built of stone masonry with a pitched, tiled roof and several small arched window openings. Within these windows, the dark silhouettes of several birds are visible resting inside.</image-desc>

<image-desc>A marginal illustration at the bottom of the page depicts two figures in 13th-century style attire engaged in falconry. On the left, a figure in a blue tunic and a reddish cap is seated and holds a hawk or falcon, appearing to assist in a procedure. On the right, a second figure in a reddish-purple tunic and a blue hat stands and appears to be actively working with the bird, perhaps performing the "seeling" (ciliare) procedure described in the text above. The bird is perched on a low stand or the lap of the first figure. The style of the figures and their clothing is characteristic of the Vatican manuscript of Frederick II's "De Arte Venandi cum Avibus".</image-desc>

<image-desc>A large illustration of a falconer on the right side of the page. He is depicted in profile facing left, wearing a blue chaperon-style hat, a calf-length violet tunic, and black hose with dark shoes. In his left hand, he holds a hooded falcon perched on his gloved fist. In his right hand, he holds a second falcon that is restrained within a 'maleolus'—a white linen tube that covers the bird's body while leaving the head and feet exposed, as described in the adjacent text.</image-desc>

<image-desc>An illustration at the bottom of the page depicts two figures in a grassy field dotted with small flowers (blue, red, and yellow). On the left, a man in a wide-brimmed hat and a long purple tunic with buttons stands in profile, extending his right hand to feed a dark falcon. The falcon is perched on the gloved left hand of the second figure on the right. This second figure is shorter and appears younger, wearing a blue cap, a red-orange tunic, and patterned hose with vertical orange and white stripes. The scene illustrates the process of "aliatio" (handling/hooding) and manning a hawk as described in the accompanying text.</image-desc>

<image-desc>A detailed medieval illustration of a falconer. He is a young man with a short bowl-cut hairstyle, wearing a blue tunic over a long orange-red garment and matching hose. He wears a heavy brown leather glove (artheca) on his left arm, which extends to his elbow; a falcon is perched on this gloved hand. He uses his right hand to grasp the bird's jesses or equipment. A brown leather bag (carnaria) with a long strap is slung across his chest. The ground beneath him is sparsely decorated with three stylized green plants.</image-desc>

In the center, a long horizontal wooden perch (prica) is supported by two A-frame trestles. On the left, a falconer in a blue tunic and red hose holds a pair of falconry gloves. <image-desc>
A detailed naturalistic illustration occupies the bottom third of the page. It depicts two falcons standing on a long horizontal wooden perch (pertica) supported by two trestle-style legs forming 'X' shapes. The falcon on the left is lighter in color with brown speckles and a striped tail, facing right. The falcon on the right has darker, more solid plumage on its back and wings with heavy barring on its breast, facing left. Both birds are tethered to the perch by thin leashes tied around the central bar. Beneath the perch, a strip of ground is decorated with stylized green grass and small red and blue flowers.
</image-desc>

<vocab>pertica, falco, mansuetus, sedile, diuerberandi, portatilis, De arte venandi cum avibus</vocab>

<image-desc>A detailed colored ink drawing at the bottom of the page depicts five falcons of varying breeds and plumage patterns (speckled brown, barred grey, and solid brown) perched on a long horizontal wooden bar. The bar is supported by three pairs of simple wooden legs. Each bird is tethered to the bar by jesses (thin leather straps) attached to its legs. The birds are shown in naturalistic poses: the leftmost faces away, while the others are shown in profile or three-quarter views, some looking back or downward.</image-desc>

<image-desc>A medieval illumination at the bottom of the page spans both columns. It features three human figures and several birds of prey. On the left, a falconer in a blue and red vertically striped tunic, blue hat, and dark boots stands holding a large brown bird on his left gloved hand. In the center, a bird of prey is perched on a low, dark wooden stand with a circular top. On the right, a second falconer is mounted on a brown horse; he wears a blue robe and a red hat and holds a similar brown bird on his left gloved hand. <image-desc>A wide rectangular illustration spans the bottom of the page. It depicts training activities in medieval falconry. On the left, a falconer wearing a red and dark blue vertically striped tunic and a white coif holds a hooded hawk on his gloved left hand. In the center, two falcons (one hooded, one unhooded) rest on a horizontal wooden pole (pertica) supported by two trestles. A second man, dressed in a blue tunic, stands behind the perch and reaches toward the birds. Portions of additional figures are visible at the right edge, where the page is bound.</image-desc>

<image-desc>
Four sequential illustrations at the bottom of the page depicting the process of unseeling and habituating a falcon. 

1. On the far left, two falconers stand together. One, wearing a long pink robe and a red hat, holds a bird on his gloved left hand while the other man, in a dark blue robe, assists by holding the bird's tail or legs.
2. In the center-left, a falconer in a blue robe and red hat is seated or slightly bent, carefully working on the head of a hawk held on his fist, likely performing the "dealuatio" (removing the threads from the eyelids).
3. In the center-right, a man in a red robe and white cap holds a tall, lit candle towards another falconer in a purple robe and red hat. The light is being introduced to a hawk held on the latter's hand to begin its habituation to light and people.
4. On the far right, a woman in a long green dress and a white coif stands in profile, holding a large hawk on her gloved hand, representing the successful manumission or training of the bird.
</image-desc>

<vocab>falco, dealuatio, mansuefieri, agrestitatem, palpebras, lumen, ho(m)i(n)is, carnib(us), noctem</vocab>

A horizontal illustration at the foot of the page depicts scenes of falconry. On the left, a man in a brown tunic, blue leggings, and a white coif stands holding a hooded falcon on his gloved left hand. In the center, another man wearing an orange-gold robe and a red coif leans over a falcon perched on a low, T-shaped wooden stand, appearing to attend to the bird. On the right, a figure in a blue robe sits facing a golden chalice or ciborium placed upon a low pedestal. The style is late medieval, characterized by simple outlines and flat color fill.

diverteret se ad altum In ceteris suis omnib_us_ diverberationib_us_ huiusmodi diverberare minus est nocitum et. Quando vero diverbertat se in longitudine brachij uersus humeru_m_ falconarius sic plicet brachium et manu_m_ ad faciem suam sic vertat se quod oporteat falconem uertere se uersus faciem. et tunc rettrahendu_m_ erit cito ad manu_m_ super qua stabat quadmodum quando diverberabat se uersus faciem. Et quando diuerberat se per longitudine_m_ manus extra manu_m_ falconarius uertat se et manu_m_ suam retrahat taliter quod per iuuamentu_m_ suu_m_ falconem oporteat esse uersus aliud latus falconarij et tunc cito retrahat falconem ad manu_m_ quadmodum in diuert_atione fit que est de uno latere ad aliud. Si autem falco diverber_at_ se uersus aliud latus ut minus noceat ei falconarius debet assentire falconi cum manu portante ipsu_m_ quam manu_m_ laxā et assentiente_m_ falconi teneat non rigidam; quod et proderit in omni diverberatione; postea debet rettrahere manu_m_ eandē suam et cito in loco in quo primo erat dum portabat falconem. et si falco diverberauerit se ad altum fiet hoc modo.

<column-break/>

assentiet cum manu et post retinebit eam quia in retrahendo falcone falco descendet facilius si si diverberatus fuerit se falco inter sursum et deorsum oportebit ut reclinet manu_m_. Nam falco conuenientius retrahet ad manu_m_ si subierit pedum; sine sunt et si diverberauerit se in deorsum per consentiet ei cum manu et deinde aliuntulum eleuet manu_m_ ut cum adiutorio ipsius manu subleuantis et attrahentis falconem melius et altius se releuet et postquam se releuauerit bumilietur in eandem manu_m_ quam subsit falconi. falco enim rediens ad sursum in deorsum conuenientius semper redit super manu_m_; quod autem dictum est de consentiendo falconi cum manu et de submittendo manu_m_ eandē; de eleuando ubi expedit et de retrahento falconem falconarius sine mora faciat quia citius poterit; mora ei in talibus trahit a se periculu_m_ consuetudinis quod dictum erat si consuerus eet portari in una manu et altera iuuar_et_ assuescere portare falconem super utramque manu_m_ quia ad uices facere diverberationes de utraque manu ad aliud latus et non ad posteriora manus. Quando vero falco diverberaverit se ad posteriora manus oportebit fal-

<image-desc>
A horizontal illustration at the bottom of the page depicts practices of falconry. It is divided into two scenes. On the left, a falconer in a blue tunic and yellow leggings stands before a T-shaped wooden perch (pertica) on which a falcon is perched; he appears to be adjusting its jesses. On the right, two falconers stand near another T-shaped perch. The man in the middle, in a blue tunic, looks toward his companion on the far right. The man on the far right, dressed in a red-orange tunic and blue leggings, holds a falcon on his gloved left hand while holding the lure (tiratorium) in his right, ready to place the bird on the perch or attract its attention. The scene is set on a green meadow with small flowering plants.
</image-desc>

<image-desc>A detailed manuscript illumination occupies the lower half of the page. It depicts a falconry scene within an architectural setting. A long horizontal wooden perch, or pertica, is mounted between a red brick wall on the far left and a tall, slender stone tower on the far right. The tower features three levels with small arched windows and is capped with a blue tiled roof. Two falconers are tending to the birds. The falconer on the left, dressed in a blue tunic and a small cap, holds the end of the perch. Two dark-feathered falcons are resting on the bar near him. The falconer on the right, wearing a brownish-purple robe with a white hood, stands on the green, flower-dotted ground and holds a red lure or piece of meat in his left hand, engaging with a third falcon perched at the right end of the bar. A faint Vatican Library watermark is visible over the illustration.</image-desc>

<image-desc>
A detailed medieval miniature depicting falconry practices. The illustration is divided into three sections set against a plain parchment background with some architectural elements. 

On the left, a falconer wearing a red-and-white tunic and a white coif stands indoors or near a wall, holding a long wooden "pertica" (perch) upon which a hooded falcon is perched. 

In the center, another falcon sits on a low T-shaped perch while a man in a red hat and a blue tunic with yellow sleeves looks on, gesturing toward the bird. 

On the right, a third man in a long blue tunic is shown in an active pose, climbing a wooden ladder that leans against a sturdy stone tower or building with crenelated tops. A falcon is perched on the edge of the building's roof. This scene illustrates the management of hawks in relation to buildings, windows, and perches as described in the text.
</image-desc>

A detailed medieval illustration occupies the bottom half of the page, showing three falconers attending to birds on a horizontal wooden perch. 
- On the left, a falconer in a purple tunic and white coif extends a gloved hand toward a falcon.

- On the right, a falconer in a blue tunic and hood looks toward a falcon on the right side of the perch.

At the bottom of the page are three pen and ink sketches illustrating falconry equipment and practices.

<language>Latin</language>
<page-type>blank</page-type>

<image-desc>
This image displays the fore-edge of a historical manuscript, identified by the bottom watermark as MS Pal. lat. 1071 from the Biblioteca Apostolica Vaticana. The view shows the edges of a thick block of parchment or paper folios held between two heavy wooden boards. The binding is secured by two brass and leather clasps. Each clasp features a brass plate attached to the wooden board with screws and pins, connected to a leather strap that wraps around to catch on the opposite board. This type of sturdy binding with clasps was common for medieval and early modern codices to prevent the parchment from warping.
</image-desc>

<vocab>bookbinding, fore-edge, wooden boards, brass clasps, leather straps, parchment, codex, Palatinus Latinus</vocab>

<image-desc>
A vertical photograph showing the fore-edge of a thick historical manuscript (Codex Palatinus latinus 1071). The tightly packed edges of the parchment leaves are visible, displaying a characteristic wavy and layered texture consistent with aged vellum. The volume is bound between two heavy wooden boards, which are partially covered with dark, mottled brown leather near the spine and corners. The image captures the physical depth and material construction of the medieval codex.
</image-desc>
